D_{Q4}(r)=\big[\boldsymbol{\hat{P}}_{n}-\boldsymbol{{P}}_{n}\big]^{\top}\big[\boldsymbol{\tilde{\chi}}_{n}(r)-\mathbf{\tilde{W}}_{n}(r)\big]
, then

\displaystyle\int_{\alpha}^{1}\big\{\boldsymbol{{\hat{P}}}_{n}^{\top}[\boldsymbol{\chi}_{n}(r){-}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{n}(\alpha){-}\frac{r{-}\alpha}{1{-}\alpha}(\boldsymbol{\chi}_{n}(1){-}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{n}(\alpha))]\big\}^{2}dr{=}\int_{\alpha}^{1}\big\{D_{Q1}(r){+}D_{Q2}(r){+}D_{Q3}(r){+}D_{Q4}(r)\big\}^{2}dr.

For
i=2,3,4
, since
\big|\int_{\alpha}^{1}\big\{D_{Q1}(r)D_{Qi}(r)\big\}dr\big|\leq\big\{\int_{\alpha}^{1}D^{2}_{Q1}(r)dr\big\}^{1/2}\big\{\int_{\alpha}^{1}D^{2}_{Qi}(r)dr\big\}^{1/2}
, it suffice to show
\int_{\alpha}^{1}D^{2}_{Qi}(r)dr=o_{p}(\int_{\alpha}^{1}D^{2}_{Q1}(r)dr)
. Note that
\int_{\alpha}^{1}D^{2}_{Q2}(r)dr\leq O_{p}(b_{n})\int_{\alpha}^{1}||\mathbf{\tilde{W}}_{n}(r)||^{2}dr
and by Lemma
D.12
and
D.13
,
\int_{\alpha}^{1}||\mathbf{\tilde{W}}_{n}(r)||^{2}dr=o_{p}(p_{n}^{1+\epsilon})
for any small
\epsilon>0
, we have
\int_{\alpha}^{1}D^{2}_{Q2}(r)dr=o_{p}(p_{n})
. Since
||\boldsymbol{{P}}_{n}||^{2}=o_{p}(p_{n}^{1+\epsilon})
for any small
\epsilon>0
and
\int_{\alpha}^{1}||\boldsymbol{\tilde{\chi}}_{n}(r)-\mathbf{\tilde{W}}_{n}(r)||^{2}dr=O_{p}(b_{n})
, we have
\int_{\alpha}^{1}D^{2}_{Q3}(r)dr=o_{p}(p_{n})
and
\int_{\alpha}^{1}D^{2}_{Q4}(r)dr=O_{p}(b_{n}^{2})
, so it suffice to show
\big\{\int_{\alpha}^{1}D^{2}_{Q1}(r)dr\big\}^{-1}=O_{P}(p_{n}^{-1})
. Note that conditioning on
\boldsymbol{{P}}_{n}
,

\int_{\alpha}^{1}D^{2}_{Q1}(r)dr\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}(1{-}\alpha)^{2}\mathbf{B}_{p_{n}}(\alpha)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{n}\mathbf{B}_{p_{n}}(\alpha)\int_{0}^{1}\big\{B(r)-rB(1)\big\}^{2}dr.

Let
H(\cdot)
be the cdf of
\int_{0}^{1}\big\{B(r)-rB(1)\big\}^{2}dr
, so for
K>0
,

\displaystyle P(p_{n}\big\{\int_{\alpha}^{1}D^{2}_{Q1}(r)dr\big\}^{-1}\geq K)=

\displaystyle P(\int_{\alpha}^{1}D^{2}_{Q1}(r)dr\leq\frac{1}{K}p_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Big\{H\Big(\frac{1}{(1{-}\alpha)^{2}K}\frac{p_{n}}{\mathbf{B}_{p_{n}}(\alpha)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{n}\mathbf{B}_{p_{n}}(\alpha)}\Big)\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle H\Big(\frac{1}{(1{-}\alpha)^{2}\sqrt{K}}\Big)+P(p_{n}\big\{\mathbf{B}_{p_{n}}(\alpha)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{n}\mathbf{B}_{p_{n}}(\alpha)\big\}^{-1}\geq\sqrt{K})

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle 0\mbox{ as }K\to\infty,

(66)

which implies
\big\{\int_{\alpha}^{1}D^{2}_{Q1}(r)dr\big\}^{-1}=O_{P}(p_{n}^{-1})
.

\qed

C.7
Proof of Theorem
6

Following the same argument as at the beginning of the proof of Theorem
3
, we treat
\{\mathbf{X}_{nt}\}_{t=1}^{n}
as
\{\mathbf{X}^{\prime}_{nt}\}_{t=1}^{n}
and assume the former is defined in the same probability space as
\mathbf{W}_{n}(r)
.

From the proof of part (a) in Appendix
C.6
, we know the numerator of
T_{n}^{(2)}(\alpha)
equals to

(1-\alpha)^{-1}\Big\{\boldsymbol{{P}}_{n}^{\top}\big[\mathbf{W}_{n}(1){-}\mathbf{W}_{n}(\alpha)\big]\Big\}^{2}(1+o_{p}(1))+\Big\{{\lfloor n\alpha\rfloor}\sqrt{\frac{n{-}{\lfloor n\alpha\rfloor}}{n}}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{D_{g}}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}\Big\}^{2}(1+o_{p}(1)).

By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,
\boldsymbol{{P}}_{n}^{\top}\big[\mathbf{W}_{n}(1){-}\mathbf{W}_{n}(\alpha)\big]\leq||\boldsymbol{{P}}_{n}||||\mathbf{W}_{n}(1){-}\mathbf{W}_{n}(\alpha)||=o_{p}(p_{n}^{1+\epsilon})
for any small
\epsilon>0
. Since
\frac{1}{\sigma_{max}}n||\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}||^{2}\leq n\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{D_{g}}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}\leq\frac{1}{\sigma_{min}}n||\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}||^{2}
, the numerator of
T_{n}^{(2)}(\alpha)
is of the same order as
n^{2}||\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}||^{4}
.

For the same reason, from the proof of part (b) in Appendix
C.6
, we know the denominator of
T_{n}^{(2)}(\alpha)
equals to

(1{-}\alpha)^{-2}\int_{\alpha}^{1}D^{2}_{Q1}(r)dr(1+o_{p}(1))+(1{-}\alpha)^{-2}\int_{\alpha}^{1}\big\{\frac{{\lfloor n\alpha\rfloor}}{\sqrt{n}}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{D_{g}}\mathbf{\tilde{W}}_{n}(r)\big\}^{2}dr(1+o_{p}(1))

By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,
\int_{\alpha}^{1}D^{2}_{Q1}(r)dr\leq||\boldsymbol{{P}}_{n}||^{2}\int_{\alpha}^{1}||\mathbf{\tilde{W}}_{n}(r)||^{2}dr=o_{p}(p_{n}^{2+\epsilon})
for any small
\epsilon>0
. Since
\int_{\alpha}^{1}\big\{{\sqrt{n}}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{D_{g}}\mathbf{\tilde{W}}_{n}(r)\big\}^{2}dr\leq\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}_{min}}n||\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}||^{2}\int_{\alpha}^{1}||\mathbf{\tilde{W}}_{n}(r)||^{2}dr=\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}_{min}}n^{2}||\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}||^{4}\frac{\int_{\alpha}^{1}||\mathbf{\tilde{W}}_{n}(r)||^{2}dr}{n||\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}||^{2}}=o_{p}(n^{2}||\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}||^{4})
and
\frac{p_{n}^{2+\epsilon}}{n^{2}||\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}||^{4}}\to 0
for
\epsilon<4\kappa
, the denominator of
T_{n}^{(2)}(\alpha)
is of order
o_{p}(n^{2}||\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n}||^{4})
and the theorem is proved.

\qed

C.8
Proof of Proposition
2

We prove this proposition for
p=1
since the case for
p>1
is analogous. For part (i), when
p=1
,
Z_{t}=(X_{t}-\mu)(X_{t+1}-\mu)
and
\hat{Z}_{t}=(X_{t}-\bar{X}_{n})(X_{t+1}-\bar{X}_{n})
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{t=1}^{{\lfloor nr\rfloor}}\hat{Z}_{t}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{t=1}^{{\lfloor nr\rfloor}}Z_{t}-(\bar{X}_{n}-\mu)\Big\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{t=1}^{{\lfloor nr\rfloor}}(X_{t+1}-\mu)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{t=1}^{{\lfloor nr\rfloor}}(X_{t}-\mu)-\frac{{\lfloor nr\rfloor}}{\sqrt{n}}(\bar{X}_{n}-\mu)\Big\}.

Since the uniform metric on
D[0,1]
is stronger than the Skorokhod metric, by Theorem 3.1 of
Billingsley 2013
, it suffice to show

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{r\in[0,1]}|(\bar{X}_{n}-\mu)\Big\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{t=1}^{{\lfloor nr\rfloor}}(X_{t+1}-\mu)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{t=1}^{{\lfloor nr\rfloor}}(X_{t}-\mu)-\frac{{\lfloor nr\rfloor}}{\sqrt{n}}(\bar{X}_{n}-\mu)\Big\}|=o_{p}(1).

(67)

By the FCLT assumption on
\{X_{t}\}
, we have
\bar{X}_{n}-\mu=o_{p}(1)
,
\sup\limits_{r\in[0,1]}|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{t=1}^{{\lfloor nr\rfloor}}(X_{t+1}-\mu)|=O_{p}(1)
,
\sup\limits_{r\in[0,1]}|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{t=1}^{{\lfloor nr\rfloor}}(X_{t}-\mu)|=O_{p}(1)
and
\sqrt{n}(\bar{X}_{n}-\mu)=O_{p}(1)
. So Equation (
67
) is proved and the proof for part (i) is complete.

For part (ii), since

\displaystyle\hat{Z}_{t}=Z_{t}+(\bar{X}_{n}-\mu)^{2}+(X_{t}+X_{t+1}-2\mu)(\bar{X}_{n}-\mu),

we have
n^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\hat{Z}_{t}=n^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{n}{Z}_{t}+(\bar{X}_{n}-\mu)^{2}+(\bar{X}_{n}+n^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{n}X_{t+1}-2\mu)(\bar{X}_{n}-\mu)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}E(Z_{1})
. Also, since

\displaystyle n^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{n}(X_{t}+X_{t+1}-2\mu)^{2}\leq n^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\big\{(X_{t}-\mu)^{2}+(X_{t+1}-\mu)^{2}\big\}=O_{P}(1),

and
\bar{X}_{n}-\mu=o_{p}(1)
, we have
n^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\hat{Z}_{t}^{2}=n^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{n}{Z}_{t}^{2}+o_{p}(1)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}E(Z_{1}^{2})
. So part (ii) is proved.

\qed

C.9
Proof of Theorem
8

Denote

\hat{h}=\argmax_{\mathbf{j}\in\{1,2,\dots,p\}}\frac{n^{-1}[\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{M}_{1,{\lfloor nb\rfloor}}]^{2}}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}\mbox{ and }\tilde{G}_{n}=\sup_{k={\lfloor nb\rfloor}+1,{\lfloor nb\rfloor}+2,\dots,n-{\lfloor nb\rfloor}-1}\frac{T_{n}(\hat{h},k)^{2}}{V_{n}(\hat{h},k)},

First to prove part (i) of Theorem
8
. Following the same argument as in the proof of Theorem
1
, we have
P(\hat{j}\neq\hat{h})\to 0
and
P(\tilde{G}_{n}\neq G_{n})\to 0
, so it suffices to prove
\tilde{G}_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}}}{{\to}}G
. By simple calculation we can see that

\displaystyle\hat{h}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}}}{{\to}}\,\argmax\limits_{j\in\{1,2,\dots,p\}}\frac{\Big[B^{(j)}(b)-(B^{(j)}(1)-B^{(j)}(1-b))\Big]^{2}}{\vartheta_{j}^{2}}=\tilde{h},

where
B^{(j)}(r)
is the
j
th coordinate of
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{1/2}\mathbf{B}_{p}(r)
. Also, let
k=\lfloor\tau n\rfloor
for some
\tau\in(b,1-b)
, by continuous mapping theorem, we have

\displaystyle T_{n}(\hat{h},k)^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}}}{{\to}}\frac{1}{1-2b}\Big\{B^{(\tilde{h})}(\tau)-B^{(\tilde{h})}(b)-\frac{\tau-b}{1-2b}(B^{(\tilde{h})}(1-b)-B^{(\tilde{h})}(b))\Big\}^{2}

\displaystyle=T(\tilde{h},\tau)^{2}

\displaystyle V_{n}(\hat{h},k)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-2b)^{2}}\Big[\int_{b}^{\tau}\big\{B^{(\tilde{h})}(s)-B^{(\tilde{h})}(b)-\frac{(s-b)}{\tau-b}(B^{(\tilde{h})}(\tau)-B^{(\tilde{h})}(b))\big\}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{\tau}^{1-b}\big\{B^{(\tilde{h})}(1-b)-B^{(\tilde{h})}(s)-\frac{1-b-s}{1-b-\tau}(B^{(\tilde{h})}(1-b)-B^{(\tilde{h})}(\tau))\big\}^{2}ds\Big]

\displaystyle=V(\tilde{h},\tau),

so the limiting distribution of
\tilde{G}_{n}
is
\sup_{\tau\in[b,1-b]}\frac{T(\tilde{h},\tau)^{2}}{V({\tilde{h}},\tau)}
. By the orthogonal increment property of Brownian motion, we have
\{B^{(j)}(r)\}_{r\in[0,b],j\in\{1,\dots,p\}}
,
\{B^{(k)}(s)-B^{(k)}(b)\}_{s\in[b,\tau],k\in\{1,\dots,p\}}
,
\{B^{(l)}(1-b)-B^{(l)}(t)\}_{t\in[\tau,1-b],l\in\{1,\dots,p\}}
and
\{B^{(m)}(x)-B^{(m)}(1-b)\}_{x\in[1-b,1],m\in\{1,\dots,p\}}
are independent, which implies that the conditional distribution of
\sup_{\tau\in[0,1]}\frac{T(\tilde{h},\tau)^{2}}{V(\tilde{h},\tau)}
given
\tilde{h}
is

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in[b,1-b]}\frac{T(\tilde{h},\tau)^{2}}{V(\tilde{h},\tau)}\Big|\tilde{h}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\sup_{\tau\in[b,1{-}b]}\frac{\frac{T(\tilde{h},\tau)^{2}}{{\Gamma_{\tilde{h}\tilde{h}}}}}{\frac{V(\tilde{h},\tau)}{{\Gamma_{\tilde{h}\tilde{h}}}}}\Big|\tilde{h}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in[b,1{-}b]}\frac{(1{-}2b)\Big[B(\tau){-}B(b){-}\frac{\tau{-}b}{1{-}2b}(B(1{-}b){-}B(b))\Big]^{2}}{\int_{b}^{\tau}\Big\{B(s){-}B(b){-}\frac{s{-}b}{\tau{-}b}(B(\tau){-}B(b))\Big\}^{2}ds+\int_{\tau}^{1{-}b}\Big\{B(1{-}b){-}B(s){-}\frac{1{-}b{-}s}{1{-}b{-}\tau}(B(1{-}b){-}B(\tau))\Big\}^{2}ds}

(68)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in[b,1{-}b]}\frac{(1{-}2b)\Big[B(\tau){-}B(b){-}\frac{\tau{-}b}{1{-}2b}(B(1{-}b){-}B(b))\Big]^{2}}{\int_{0}^{\tau{-}b}\Big\{B(s+b){-}B(b){-}\frac{s}{\tau{-}b}(B(\tau){-}B(b))\Big\}^{2}ds+\int_{\tau{-}b}^{1{-}2b}\Big\{B(1{-}b){-}B(s+b){-}\frac{1{-}2b{-}s}{1{-}b{-}\tau}(B(1{-}b){-}B(\tau))\Big\}^{2}ds}

(69)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in[b,1{-}b]}\frac{(1{-}2b)\Big[B(\tau{-}b){-}\frac{\tau{-}b}{1{-}2b}(B(1{-}2b))\Big]^{2}}{\int_{0}^{\tau{-}b}\Big\{B(s){-}\frac{s}{\tau{-}b}B(\tau{-}b)\Big\}^{2}ds+\int_{\tau{-}b}^{1{-}2b}\Big\{B(1{-}2b){-}B(s){-}\frac{1{-}2b{-}s}{1{-}b{-}\tau}(B(1{-}2b){-}B(\tau{-}b))\Big\}^{2}ds}

(70)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[0,1]}\frac{(1{-}2b)\Big[B((1{-}2b)r){-}rB(1{-}2b)\Big]^{2}}{\int_{0}^{(1{-}2b)r}\Big\{B(s){-}\frac{s}{(1{-}2b)r}B((1{-}2b)r)\Big\}^{2}ds+\int_{(1{-}2b)r}^{1{-}2b}\Big\{B(1{-}2b){-}B(s){-}\frac{1{-}2b{-}s}{(1{-}2b)(1{-}r)}(B(1{-}2b){-}B((1{-}2b)r))\Big\}^{2}ds}

(71)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[0,1]}\frac{\Big[B(r){-}rB(1)\Big]^{2}}{\int_{0}^{r}\Big\{B(s){-}\frac{s}{r}B(r)\Big\}^{2}ds+\int_{r}^{1}\Big\{B(1){-}B(s){-}\frac{1{-}s}{1{-}r}(B(1){-}B(r))\Big\}^{2}ds}

(72)

For Equation (
68
), we used the fact that for any fixed
j
,
\frac{T(j,\tau)^{2}}{V(j,\tau)}
is independent of
\tilde{h}
. For Equation (
69
) we used the change of variable property for integration. For Equation (
70
) we used the property
\{B(r+b)-B(b):r\in[0,1-2b]\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\{B(r):r\in[0,1-2b]\}
. For Equation (
71
) we substitute
\tau-b
with
(1-2b)r
and for Equation (
72
) we used the change of variable property for integration and the fact
\{B((1-2b)r):r\in[0,1]\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\{\sqrt{1-2b}B(r):r\in[0,1]\}
.

To prove part (ii).1 of Theorem
8
, note that there exist a sequence
\{j_{n}\}\in\{1,2,\dots,p\}
such that
\sqrt{n}|\Delta_{n}^{j_{n}}|\to\infty
. By the definition of
\hat{j}
we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\lfloor nb\rfloor}^{-1}\max\limits_{j=1,2,\dots,p}[\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{M}_{1,{\lfloor nb\rfloor}}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{n})]^{2}+\frac{2}{\sqrt{{\lfloor nb\rfloor}}}\max\limits_{j=1,2,\dots,p}|\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{M}_{1,{\lfloor nb\rfloor}}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{n})|\sqrt{{\lfloor nb\rfloor}}|\Delta_{n}^{\hat{j}}|+{\lfloor nb\rfloor}[\Delta_{n}^{\hat{j}}]^{2}}{\min\limits_{j=1,2,\dots,p}\hat{\vartheta}_{j}^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{{\lfloor nb\rfloor}^{-1}[\mathbf{e}_{\hat{j}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{M}_{1,{\lfloor nb\rfloor}}]^{2}}{\hat{\vartheta}_{\hat{j}}^{2}}\geq\frac{{\lfloor nb\rfloor}^{-1}[\mathbf{e}_{j_{n}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{M}_{1,{\lfloor nb\rfloor}}]^{2}}{\hat{\vartheta}_{j_{n}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{{\lfloor nb\rfloor}^{-1}\min\limits_{j=1,2,\dots,p}[\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{M}_{1,{\lfloor nb\rfloor}}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{n})]^{2}-\frac{2}{\sqrt{{\lfloor nb\rfloor}}}\max\limits_{j=1,2,\dots,p}|\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{M}_{1,{\lfloor nb\rfloor}}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{n})|\sqrt{{\lfloor nb\rfloor}}|\Delta_{n}^{j_{n}}|+{\lfloor nb\rfloor}[\Delta_{n}^{j_{n}}]^{2}}{\max\limits_{j=1,2,\dots,p}\hat{\vartheta}_{j}^{2}}.

(73)

Since
\{\max\limits_{j=1,2,\dots,p}\hat{\vartheta}_{j}^{2}\}^{-1}
,
\frac{2}{\sqrt{{\lfloor nb\rfloor}}}\max\limits_{j=1,2,\dots,p}|\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{M}_{1,{\lfloor nb\rfloor}}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{n})|
and
{\lfloor nb\rfloor}^{-1}\min\limits_{j=1,2,\dots,p}[\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{M}_{1,{\lfloor nb\rfloor}}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{n})]^{2}
are all of the order
O_{p}(1)
, the right hand side of Inequality (
73
) diverges to
\infty
in probability. Since
\max\limits_{j=1,2,\dots,p}[\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{M}_{1,{\lfloor nb\rfloor}}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{n})]^{2}
and
\{\min\limits_{j=1,2,\dots,p}\hat{\vartheta}_{j}^{2}\}^{-1}
are also of order
O_{p}(1)
, we have that
\sqrt{n}|\Delta_{n}^{\hat{j}}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}\infty
. Since

\displaystyle G_{n}\geq

\displaystyle\frac{T_{n}(\hat{j},k^{\ast})^{2}}{V_{n}(\hat{j},k^{\ast})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Big[\frac{1}{\sqrt{n-2{\lfloor nb\rfloor}}}\sum_{t={\lfloor nb\rfloor}+1}^{k^{\ast}}\big\{(X^{\hat{j}}_{t}-\mu_{t}^{\hat{j}})-\frac{1}{n-2{\lfloor nb\rfloor}}\sum_{i={\lfloor nb\rfloor}+1}^{n-{\lfloor nb\rfloor}}(X^{\hat{j}}_{i}-\mu_{i}^{\hat{j}})\big\}-\frac{(k^{\ast}-{\lfloor nb\rfloor})(n-{\lfloor nb\rfloor}-k^{\ast})\Delta_{n}^{\hat{j}}}{(n-2{\lfloor nb\rfloor})^{3/2}}\Big]^{2}}{V_{n}(\hat{j},k^{\ast})}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\min\limits_{j=1,2,\dots,p}[A(j,k^{\ast})]^{2}-2\max\limits_{j=1,2,\dots,p}|A(j,k^{\ast})||C_{n}|+C_{n}^{2}}{\max\limits_{j=1,2,\dots,p}V_{n}(j,k^{\ast})},

(74)

where
A(j,k^{\ast})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n-2{\lfloor nb\rfloor}}}\sum_{t={\lfloor nb\rfloor}+1}^{k^{\ast}}\big\{(X^{j}_{t}-\mu_{t}^{j})-\frac{1}{n-2{\lfloor nb\rfloor}}\sum_{i={\lfloor nb\rfloor}+1}^{n-{\lfloor nb\rfloor}}(X^{j}_{i}-\mu_{i}^{j})\big\}
and
C_{n}=\frac{(k^{\ast}-{\lfloor nb\rfloor})(n-{\lfloor nb\rfloor}-k^{\ast})\Delta_{n}^{\hat{j}}}{(n-2{\lfloor nb\rfloor})^{3/2}}
. Since
[\max_{j=1,2,\dots,p}V_{n}(j,k^{\ast})]^{-1}
,
\min_{j=1,2,\dots,p}[A(j,k^{\ast})]^{2}
and
\max_{j=1,2,\dots,p}|A(j,k^{\ast})|
are all of order
O_{p}(1)
and
|C_{n}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}\infty
, the right hand side of Inequality (
74
) diverges to
\infty
in probability and part (ii).1 of Theorem
8
is proved.

To prove part (ii).2 and (ii).3, for
r\in[0,1]
define
\mathbf{F}_{n}(r)=n^{-1/2}\sum_{t=1}^{\lfloor nr\rfloor}(\boldsymbol{X}_{t}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t})
,
\mathbf{\tilde{F}}_{n}(r)=n^{-1/2}\sum_{t=1}^{k^{\ast}}(\boldsymbol{X}_{t}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t})+n^{-1/2}\sum_{t=k^{\ast}+1}^{{\lfloor nr\rfloor}}(\boldsymbol{X}_{t}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{n})
and
\mathbf{H}_{n}(r)=n^{-1/2}\sum_{t=k^{\ast}+1}^{\lfloor nr\rfloor}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{n}
(For any nonnegative integer
a,b
and sequence
\{y_{t}\}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we set
\sum_{t=a}^{b}y_{t}=0
if
a<b
). By Assumption
9
,
\mathbf{F}_{n}(r)=\mathbf{\tilde{F}}_{n}(r)-\mathbf{H}_{n}(r)\Rightarrow\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{1/2}\mathbf{B}_{p}(r)
. Since
\mathbf{H}_{n}(r)
converges to
\mathbf{H}(r)=(r-r_{0})\mathbf{c}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq r_{0}}
in the uniform metric and
\mathbf{H}(r)
is a continuous function in
D^{p}[0,1]
, we have

\mathbf{\tilde{F}}_{n}(r)\Rightarrow\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{1/2}\mathbf{B}_{p}(r)+\mathbf{H}(r).

Define
H_{j}(r)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Gamma_{jj}}}\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{H}(r)
,
C(r)=B(r)+H_{j}(r)-B(b)
,
C^{\prime}(r)=B(r)+H_{j}(r)
,
D(r)=B(r-b)+H_{j}(r)
,
a_{s}=\frac{s}{(1-2b)r}
and
b_{s}=\frac{1-2b-s}{(1-2b)(1-r)}
. From this we can use the same conjecture as in the proof of part (i) of this theorem (by replacing
\mathbf{F}_{n}(r)
and
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{1/2}\mathbf{B}_{p}(r)
with
\mathbf{\tilde{F}}_{n}(r)
and
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{1/2}\mathbf{B}_{p}(r)+\mathbf{H}(r)
) and derive the conditional distribution

\displaystyle G^{\ast}\big|_{j^{\ast}=j}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[b,1{-}b]}\frac{(1{{-}}2b)\Big\{C(r){-}\frac{r{-}b}{1{-}2b}(C(1-b))\Big\}^{2}}{\int_{b}^{r}\Big\{C(s){-}\frac{s{-}b}{r{-}b}(C(r))\Big\}^{2}ds{+}\int_{r}^{1{-}b}\Big\{C^{\prime}(1-b){-}C^{\prime}(s){-}\frac{1{-}b{-}s}{1{-}b{-}r}(C^{\prime}(1-b){-}C^{\prime}(r))\Big\}^{2}ds}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[b,1{-}b]}\frac{(1{-}2b)\Big\{C(r){-}\frac{r{-}b}{1{-}2b}C(1-b)\Big\}^{2}}{\int_{0}^{r{-}b}\Big\{C(s+b){-}\frac{s}{r{-}b}C(r)\Big\}^{2}ds{+}\int_{r{-}b}^{1{-}2b}\Big\{C^{\prime}(1-b){-}C^{\prime}(s+b){-}\frac{1{-}2b{-}s}{1{-}b{-}r}(C^{\prime}(1-b){-}C^{\prime}(r))\Big\}^{2}ds}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[b,1{-}b]}\frac{(1{-}2b)\Big\{D(r){-}\frac{r{-}b}{1{-}2b}D(1-b)\Big\}^{2}}{\int_{0}^{r{-}b}\Big\{D(s+b){-}\frac{s}{r{-}b}D(r)\Big\}^{2}ds{+}\int_{r{-}b}^{1{-}2b}\Big\{D(1-b){-}D(s+b){-}\frac{1{-}2b{-}s}{1{-}b{-}r}(D(1-b){-}D(r))\Big\}^{2}ds}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[b,1{-}b]}\frac{(1{-}2b)\big\{D((1{-}2b)r{+}b){-}rD(1{-}b)\big\}^{2}}{\int\limits_{0}^{(1{-}2b)r}\big\{D(s{+}b){-}a_{s}D((1{-}2b)r{+}b)\big\}^{2}ds{+}\int\limits_{(1{-}2b)r}^{1{-}2b}\big\{D(1{-}b){-}D(s{+}b){-}b_{s}(D(1{-}b){-}D((1{-}2b)r{+}b))\big\}^{2}ds}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[b,1{-}b]}\frac{\Big\{D((1{-}2b)r{+}b){-}rD(1{-}b)\Big\}^{2}}{\int\limits_{0}^{r}\Big\{D((1{-}2b)s{+}b){-}\frac{s}{r}D((1{-}2b)r{+}b)\Big\}^{2}ds{+}\int\limits_{r}^{1}\Big\{D(1{-}b){-}D((1{-}2b)s{+}b){-}\frac{1{-}s}{1{-}r}(D(1{-}b){-}D((1{-}2b)r{+}b))\Big\}^{2}ds}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in[0,1]}\frac{\Big\{B^{\prime}(r)-rB^{\prime}(1)\Big\}^{2}}{\int_{0}^{r}\Big\{B^{\prime}(s)-\frac{s}{r}B^{\prime}(r)\Big\}^{2}ds+\int_{r}^{1}\Big\{B^{\prime}(1)-B^{\prime}(s)-\frac{1-s}{1-r}(B^{\prime}(1)-B^{\prime}(r))\Big\}^{2}ds}

So part (ii).2 and (ii).3 are proved.

\qed

C.10
Proof of Theorem
7

Following the same argument as at the beginning of the proof of Theorem
3
, we treat
\{\mathbf{X}_{nt}\}_{t=1}^{n}
as
\{\mathbf{X}^{\prime}_{nt}\}_{t=1}^{n}
and assume the former is defined in the same probability space as
\mathbf{W}_{n}(r)
. As in the proofs of Theorem
3
and Theorem
5
, denote the statistics defined using the Brownian motion as

\displaystyle\hat{j}^{(W)}_{n}

\displaystyle=\argmax_{j=1,2,\dots,p_{n}}\frac{[{W}_{nj}(\alpha)]^{2}}{\sigma_{nj}^{2}}

\displaystyle T^{(W)}_{n}(\alpha,j)

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-\alpha)[{W}_{nj}(1)-{W}_{nj}(\alpha)]^{2}}{\int_{\alpha}^{1}[W_{nj}(s)-W_{nj}(\alpha)-\frac{s-\alpha}{1-\alpha}(W_{nj}(1)-W_{nj}(\alpha))]^{2}ds}.

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{{P}}_{n}

\displaystyle=(\frac{W_{n1}(\alpha)}{\sigma_{n1}},\dots,\frac{W_{np_{n}}(\alpha)}{\sigma_{np_{n}}})^{\top}

\displaystyle Q^{(W)}_{n}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\frac{(1{-}\alpha)\Big\{\boldsymbol{{P}}_{n}^{\top}\big[\mathbf{W}_{n}(1){-}\mathbf{W}_{n}(\alpha)\big]\Big\}^{2}}{\int_{\alpha}^{1}\big\{\boldsymbol{{P}}_{n}^{\top}[\mathbf{W}_{n}(r){-}\mathbf{W}_{n}(\alpha)-\frac{r{-}\alpha}{1{-}\alpha}(\mathbf{W}_{n}(1){-}\mathbf{W}_{n}(\alpha))]\big\}^{2}dr}.

In the following proof we use
C
to denote a generic positive constant whose value might vary from line to line.
We have shown that
T^{(D)}_{n}(\alpha,\hat{j}_{n})-T^{(W)}_{n}(\alpha,\hat{j}^{(W)}_{n})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}0
and
Q^{(D)}_{n}(\alpha)-Q^{(W)}_{n}(\alpha)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}0
, which implies, by Slutsky’s Theorem,

\big(T^{(D)}_{n}(\alpha,\hat{j}_{n}),Q^{(D)}_{n}(\alpha)\big)-\big(T^{(W)}_{n}(\alpha,\hat{j}^{(W)}_{n}),Q^{(W)}_{n}(\alpha)\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}0.

So it suffice to show

\displaystyle\Big|P\big(T^{(W)}_{n}(\alpha,\hat{j}^{(W)}_{n})\in A;Q^{(W)}_{n}(\alpha)\in B\big)-P\big(T^{(W)}_{n}(\alpha,\hat{j}^{(W)}_{n})\in A\big)P\big(Q^{(W)}_{n}(\alpha)\in B\big)\Big|\to 0

(75)

for any
A,B\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R})
. From Section 3.3 in
Chung 2000
we know that a sufficient condition for Equation (
75
) to hold is

\displaystyle\Big|P\big(T^{(W)}_{n}(\alpha,\hat{j}^{(W)}_{n})\leq a;Q^{(W)}_{n}(\alpha)\leq b\big)-P\big(T^{(W)}_{n}(\alpha,\hat{j}^{(W)}_{n})\leq a\big)P\big(Q^{(W)}_{n}(\alpha)\leq b\big)\Big|\to 0

(76)

for any
a,b,\in(0,\infty)
. Let
\mathcal{B}([0,\alpha])
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
\{\mathbf{W}_{n}(r)\}_{r\in[0,\alpha]}
and
\mathcal{B}([\alpha,1])
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
\{\mathbf{W}_{n}(r)-\mathbf{W}_{n}(\alpha)\}_{r\in[\alpha,1]}
. It is clear that
\mathcal{B}([0,\alpha])
and
\mathcal{B}([\alpha,1])
are independent and
\hat{j}^{(W)}_{n},\boldsymbol{{P}}_{n}
are
\mathcal{B}([0,\alpha])
measurable. Note that conditioning on
\hat{j}^{(W)}_{n}
and
\boldsymbol{{P}}_{n}
,

\displaystyle\{(W_{n\hat{j}^{(W)}_{n}}(r){-}W_{n\hat{j}^{(W)}_{n}}(\alpha),\boldsymbol{{P}}_{n}^{\top}(\mathbf{W}_{n}(r){-}\mathbf{W}_{n}(\alpha))^{\top}\}_{r\in[\alpha,1]}{=}

\displaystyle\{(\tilde{W}_{1}(r{-}\alpha),\tilde{W}_{2}(r{-}\alpha))^{\top}\}_{r\in[\alpha,1]}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\Sigma_{1}^{1/2}\{(B_{1}(r{-}\alpha),B_{2}(r{-}\alpha))^{\top}\}_{r\in[\alpha,1]},

where
\{(B_{1}(r),B_{2}(r))^{\top}\}_{r\in[0,\infty)}
is a standard bivariate Brownian motion,

\Sigma_{1}=\begin{bmatrix}\varsigma_{1}^{2}&\varrho\varsigma_{1}\varsigma_{2}\\
\varrho\varsigma_{1}\varsigma_{2}&\varsigma_{2}^{2}\end{bmatrix},

\varsigma_{1}^{2}=\mathbf{e}_{\hat{j}^{(W)}_{n}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{n}\mathbf{e}_{\hat{j}^{(W)}_{n}}
,
\varsigma_{2}^{2}=\boldsymbol{{P}}_{n}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{n}\boldsymbol{{P}}_{n}
and
\varrho=\mathbf{e}_{\hat{j}^{(W)}_{n}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{n}\boldsymbol{{P}}_{n}/(\varsigma_{1}\varsigma_{2})
. Let
\Sigma_{2}=diag\{\varsigma_{1}^{2},\varsigma_{2}^{2}\}
, then we have
||\Sigma_{2}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{1}\Sigma_{2}^{-1/2}-I_{2}||_{F}=\sqrt{2}\varrho
, where
||\cdot||_{F}
denote the Frobenius norm.

Denote
N(W)=\int_{0}^{1}(W(r)-rW(1))^{2}dr
, and let
\mathcal{\tilde{B}}
be the
\sigma
algebra generated by
\{\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho,N(\tilde{W}_{1}),N(\tilde{W}_{2})\}
, then the left hand side of Equation (
76
) can be written as

\displaystyle\Big|P\big(T^{(W)}_{n}(\alpha,\hat{j}^{(W)}_{n})\leq a;Q^{(W)}_{n}(\alpha)\leq b\big)-P\big(T^{(W)}_{n}(\alpha,\hat{j}^{(W)}_{n})\leq a\big)P\big(Q^{(W)}_{n}(\alpha)\leq b\big)\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Bigg|E\Bigg\{P\Big(\frac{\tilde{W}^{2}_{1}(1)}{N(\tilde{W}_{1})}\leq a;\frac{\tilde{W}^{2}_{2}(1)}{N(\tilde{W}_{2})}\leq b\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big){-}P(U_{1}\leq a)P(U_{1}\leq b)\Bigg\}\Bigg|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Bigg|E\Bigg\{E\Big\{P\Big(\frac{\tilde{W}^{2}_{1}(1)}{N(\tilde{W}_{1})}\leq a;\frac{\tilde{W}^{2}_{2}(1)}{N(\tilde{W}_{2})}\leq b\Big|\mathcal{\tilde{B}}\Big){-}P\Big(\frac{\tilde{W}^{2}_{1}(1)}{N(\tilde{W}_{1})}\leq a\Big|\mathcal{\tilde{B}}\Big)P\Big(\frac{\tilde{W}^{2}_{2}(1)}{N(\tilde{W}_{2})}\leq b\Big|\mathcal{\tilde{B}}\Big)\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt{+}E\Big\{P\Big(\frac{\tilde{W}^{2}_{1}(1)}{N(\tilde{W}_{1})}\leq a\Big|\mathcal{\tilde{B}}\Big)P\Big(\frac{\tilde{W}^{2}_{2}(1)}{N(\tilde{W}_{2})}\leq b\Big|\mathcal{\tilde{B}}\Big)\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\}{-}P(U_{1}\leq a)P(U_{1}\leq b)\Bigg\}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle E|I^{(1)}|+E|I^{(2)}|.

Here

\displaystyle E|I^{(1)}|=

\displaystyle E\Bigg|E\Big\{P\Big(\frac{\tilde{W}^{2}_{1}(1)}{N(\tilde{W}_{1})}\leq a;\frac{\tilde{W}^{2}_{2}(1)}{N(\tilde{W}_{2})}\leq b\Big|\mathcal{\tilde{B}}\Big){-}P\Big(\frac{\tilde{W}^{2}_{1}(1)}{N(\tilde{W}_{1})}\leq a\Big|\mathcal{\tilde{B}}\Big)P\Big(\frac{\tilde{W}^{2}_{2}(1)}{N(\tilde{W}_{2})}\leq b\Big|\mathcal{\tilde{B}}\Big)\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle E\Bigg\{E\Big\{\Bigg|P\Big(\frac{\tilde{W}^{2}_{1}(1)}{N(\tilde{W}_{1})}\leq a;\frac{\tilde{W}^{2}_{2}(1)}{N(\tilde{W}_{2})}\leq b\Big|\mathcal{\tilde{B}}\Big){-}P\Big(\frac{\tilde{W}^{2}_{1}(1)}{N(\tilde{W}_{1})}\leq a\Big|\mathcal{\tilde{B}}\Big)P\Big(\frac{\tilde{W}^{2}_{2}(1)}{N(\tilde{W}_{2})}\leq b\Big|\mathcal{\tilde{B}}\Big)\Bigg|\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3\sqrt{2}E|\varrho|,

(77)

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
D.21
and the fact that
\{N(\tilde{W}_{1}),N(\tilde{W}_{2})\}
are independent of
\{\tilde{W}_{2}(1),\tilde{W}_{1}(1)\}
. To show
E|\varrho|\to 0
, note that
|\varrho|\leq 1
and according to part (a) of the proof of Theorem
5
,
p_{n}\varsigma_{2}^{-2}=O_{p}(1)
and
\varsigma_{2}^{2}=\mathbf{B}_{p_{n}}(\alpha)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{n}\mathbf{B}_{p_{n}}(\alpha)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}{\alpha}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{p_{n}}\lambda_{i}\chi_{i}
where
\chi_{i}
are i.i.d chi-square distributed with one degree of freedom. So it suffice to show
p_{n}^{-1}(\mathbf{e}_{\hat{j}^{(W)}_{n}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{n}\boldsymbol{{P}}_{n})^{2}/\varsigma_{1}^{2}=o_{p}(1)
. Since

\displaystyle(\mathbf{e}_{\hat{j}^{(W)}_{n}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{n}\boldsymbol{{P}}_{n})^{2}/\varsigma_{1}^{2}\leq\max_{j=1,2,\dots,p_{n}}\Big(\frac{\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{n}^{1/2}}{\sqrt{\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{n}\mathbf{e}_{j}}}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{n}^{1/2}\mathbf{B}_{p_{n}}(\alpha)\Big)^{2}\leq\lambda_{p_{n}}\max_{j=1,2,\dots,p_{n}}Z_{j},

where
Z_{j}=\Big(\frac{\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{n}^{1/2}}{\sqrt{\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{n}\mathbf{e}_{j}}}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{n}^{1/2}\mathbf{B}_{p_{n}}(\alpha)\Big)^{2}/\big(\frac{\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{n}^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{n}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{n}^{1/2}\mathbf{e}_{j}}{\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{n}\mathbf{e}_{j}}\big)
follows chi-square distribution with one degree of freedom. From Lemma
D.13
, we know
\max_{j=1,2,\dots,p_{n}}Z_{j}=o_{p}(p_{n}^{\epsilon})
for any
\epsilon>0
, so
E|\varrho|\to 0
follows from Assumption
6
.

Next, we show

E|I^{(2)}|=E\Bigg|E\Big\{P\Big(\frac{\tilde{W}^{2}_{1}(1)}{N(\tilde{W}_{1})}\leq a\Big|\mathcal{\tilde{B}}\Big)P\Big(\frac{\tilde{W}^{2}_{2}(1)}{N(\tilde{W}_{2})}\leq b\Big|\mathcal{\tilde{B}}\Big)\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\}{-}P(U_{1}\leq a)P(U_{1}\leq b)\Bigg|\to 0.

Note that conditioning on
\{\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\}
,
\{(\tilde{W}_{1}(r),\tilde{W}_{2}(r))^{\top}\}_{r\in[0,1]}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\{(\varsigma_{1}B_{1}(r),\varrho\varsigma_{2}B_{1}(r){+}\sqrt{1{-}\varrho^{2}}\varsigma_{2}B_{2}(r))^{\top}\}_{r\in[0,1]}
. Denote
a(\varrho)=\varrho^{2}bN(B_{1}){-}\varrho^{2}bN(B_{2})+b\varrho\sqrt{1{-}\varrho^{2}}\tilde{N}(B_{1},B_{2})
where
\tilde{N}(B_{1},B_{2})=2\int_{0}^{1}(B_{1}(r)-rB_{1}(1))(B_{2}(r)-rB_{2}(1))dr
, we have

\displaystyle E\Big\{P\Big(\frac{\tilde{W}^{2}_{1}(1)}{N(\tilde{W}_{1})}\leq a\Big|\mathcal{\tilde{B}}\Big)P\Big(\frac{\tilde{W}^{2}_{2}(1)}{N(\tilde{W}_{2})}\leq b\Big|\mathcal{\tilde{B}}\Big)\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Big\{\mathcal{Q}\big(aN(\tilde{W}_{1})/\varsigma_{1}^{2}\big)\mathcal{Q}\big(bN(\tilde{W}_{2})/\varsigma_{2}^{2}\big)\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Big\{\mathcal{Q}\big(aN(B_{1})\big)\mathcal{Q}\big(bN(\varrho B_{1}{+}\sqrt{1{-}\varrho^{2}}B_{2})\big)\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Big\{\mathcal{Q}\big(aN(B_{1})\big)\mathcal{Q}\big(bN(B_{2}){+}a(\varrho)\big)\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Big\{\mathcal{Q}\big(aN(B_{1})\big)\mathcal{Q}\big(bN(B_{2})\big)\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\}{+}E\Big\{\mathcal{Q}\big(aN(B_{1})\big)\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\big(bN(B_{2})+\xi a(\varrho)\big)a(\varrho)\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\},

where
\mathcal{Q}(\cdot),\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}(\cdot)
are the cdf and pdf of chi-square distribution with one degree of freedom and
\xi\in(0,1)
. Note that
E\Big\{\mathcal{Q}\big(aN(B_{1})\big)\mathcal{Q}\big(bN(B_{2})\big)\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\}=P(U_{1}\leq a)P(U_{1}\leq b)
, so we have

\displaystyle E|I^{(2)}|=

\displaystyle E\Bigg|E\Big\{\mathcal{Q}\big(aN(B_{1})\big)\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\big(bN(B_{2})+\xi a(\varrho)\big)a(\varrho)\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle E\Bigg\{E\Big\{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\big(bN(B_{2})+\xi a(\varrho)\big)|a(\varrho)|\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\}\Bigg\}

(78)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CE\Bigg\{E\Big\{\big(bN(B_{2})+\xi a(\varrho)\big)^{-1/2}|a(\varrho)|\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\}\Bigg\}

(79)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CE\Bigg\{\sqrt{E\Big\{\big(bN(B_{2})+\xi a(\varrho)\big)^{-1}\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\}E\Big\{|a(\varrho)|^{2}\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\}}\Bigg\}.

(80)

In the above display, Equation (
78
) follows from the fact that
|\mathcal{Q}\big(aN(B_{1})\big)|\leq 1
, Equation (
79
) follows since
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}(x)\leq Cx^{-1/2}
for any
x>0
and Equation (
80
) follows from Holder’s Inequality.

Note that
|\tilde{N}(B_{1},B_{2})|\leq 2(N(B_{1})N(B_{2}))^{1/2}
and from Lemma
D.12
and
Tolmatz 2002
, we know that
N(B_{1}),N(B_{2})
has finite
z
-th moment for any
z\in\mathbb{Z}
. Hence we have
E\Big\{|a(\varrho)|^{2}\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\}\leq C\varrho^{2}
.
Note that
bN(B_{2})+a(\varrho)=bN(\varrho B_{1}{+}\sqrt{1{-}\varrho^{2}}B_{2})>0
and
bN(B_{2})>0
, which implies
\big(bN(B_{2})+\xi a(\varrho)\big)^{-1}\leq\big(bN(B_{2})+a(\varrho)\big)^{-1}
when
a(\varrho)<0
and
\big(bN(B_{2})+\xi a(\varrho)\big)^{-1}\leq\big(bN(B_{2})\big)^{-1}
when
a(\varrho)>0
. This implies that
\big(bN(B_{2})+\xi a(\varrho)\big)^{-1}\leq\big(bN(B_{2})+a(\varrho)\big)^{-1}{+}\big(bN(B_{2})\big)^{-1}
, which yields

\displaystyle E\Big\{\big(bN(B_{2})+\xi a(\varrho)\big)^{-1}\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\}\leq

\displaystyle E\Big\{\big(bN(B_{2})+a(\varrho)\big)^{-1}\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\}+E\Big\{\big(bN(B_{2})\big)^{-1}\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Big\{\big(bN(\varrho B_{1}{+}\sqrt{1{-}\varrho^{2}}B_{2})\big)^{-1}\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\}+C

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Big\{\big(bN(B_{1})\big)^{-1}\Big|\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varrho\Big\}+C

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2C.

This implies
E|I^{(2)}|\leq CE|\varrho|\to 0
and the theorem is proved.

\qed

Appendix D
Auxiliary Lemmas and Its Proofs

Lemma 1

Let
\mathbf{B}_{p}(r)=(B^{1}(r),\dots,B^{p}(r))^{\top}
be a
p
-dimensional standard Brownian motion and
\mathbf{W}_{p}(r)=\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{1/2}\mathbf{B}_{p}(r)
where
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}
is a positive definite matrix. For any
\mathbf{x}(r)=(x^{1}(r),\dots,x^{p}(r))^{\top}\in D^{p}[0,1]
, define

\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x})=\argmax_{j=1,2,\dots,p}\frac{\big\{x^{j}(\alpha)\big\}^{2}}{a_{j}},

where
\{a_{1},a_{2}\dots,a_{j}\}
are some positive constants. Then both
\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x})
and the functional
F(\cdot):D^{p}[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
defined as

\displaystyle F(\mathbf{x})=\frac{\big\{x^{\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x})}(1)-x^{\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x})}(\alpha)\big\}^{2}}{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}\int_{\alpha}^{1}\big\{x^{\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x})}(r)-\frac{1-r}{1-\alpha}x^{\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x})}(\alpha)-\frac{r-\alpha}{1-\alpha}x^{\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x})}(1)\big\}^{2}dr}

(81)

for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
are continuous a.e. with respect to the probability measure induced by
\mathbf{W}_{p}(r)
.

Proof D.9

(Proof of Lemma
1
)
.

Denote
C^{p}[0,1]
as the set of
\mathbb{R}^{p}
-valued continuous functions defined on
[0,1]
and let

C_{h}=\Big\{\mathbf{x}(r)\in C^{p}[0,1]:\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x})\mbox{ is unique and }\frac{1}{1{-}\alpha}\int_{\alpha}^{1}\big\{x^{\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x})}(r){-}\frac{1{-}r}{1{-}\alpha}x^{\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x})}(\alpha){-}\frac{r{-}\alpha}{1{-}\alpha}x^{\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x})}(1)\big\}^{2}dr{>}0\Big\}.

Then we have
P(\mathbf{W}_{p}(r)\in C_{h})=1
and, according to
Billingsley 2013
, p124, it suffices to show that for any
\{\mathbf{x}_{n}(r)\}\subset D^{p}[0,1]
and
\mathbf{x}(r)\in C_{h}
, we have
\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x}_{n})\to\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x})
and
F(\mathbf{x}_{n})\to F(\mathbf{x})
if
\rho(\mathbf{x}_{n},\mathbf{x})\to 0
where
\rho(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})=\max_{j=1,2,\dots,p}\sup_{r\in[0,1]}|x^{j}(r)-y^{j}(r)|
for any
\mathbf{x}(r),\mathbf{y}(r)\in D^{p}[0,1]
.

If
\rho(\mathbf{x}_{n},\mathbf{x})\to 0
and
\mathbf{x}(r)\in C_{h}
, then we have
\max_{j=1,2,\dots,p}\big|\frac{\big\{x_{n}^{j}(\alpha)\big\}^{2}}{a_{j}}{-}\frac{\big\{x^{j}(\alpha)\big\}^{2}}{a_{j}}\big|\to 0
and
\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x}_{n})=\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x})
for all
n
large enough. Assume
\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x})=j
, then we have

\displaystyle\big\{x_{n}^{j}(1)-x_{n}^{j}(\alpha)\big\}^{2}\to

\displaystyle\big\{x^{j}(1)-x^{j}(\alpha)\big\}^{2}

\displaystyle\int_{\alpha}^{1}\big\{x_{n}^{j}(r)-\frac{1-r}{1-\alpha}x_{n}^{j}(\alpha)-\frac{r-\alpha}{1-\alpha}x_{n}^{j}(1)\big\}^{2}dr\to

\displaystyle\int_{\alpha}^{1}\big\{x^{j}(r)-\frac{1-r}{1-\alpha}x^{j}(\alpha)-\frac{r-\alpha}{1-\alpha}x^{j}(1)\big\}^{2}dr>0

Since
g(x,y):\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
defined as
g(x,y)=x/y
is continuous on
\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:y\neq 0\}
, we have
F(\mathbf{x}_{n})\to F(\mathbf{x})
and the lemma is proved.

Lemma D.10
.

Let
\lambda_{1}(\mathbf{H})\leq\lambda_{2}(\mathbf{H})\leq\cdots\leq\lambda_{n}(\mathbf{H})
be the eigenvalues of an
n
-dimensional matrix
\mathbf{H}
, then we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(\mathbf{B})\lambda_{n}(\mathbf{A})\leq\lambda_{n}(\mathbf{AB})\leq\lambda_{n}(\mathbf{B})\lambda_{n}(\mathbf{A})

(82)

for any positive definite matrices
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{B}
.

Proof D.11

(Proof of Lemma
D.10
)
.

From Theorem A.7.1 in
Seber and
Lee 2003
,
\lambda_{n}(\mathbf{AB})=\lambda_{n}(\mathbf{B}^{1/2}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}^{1/2})
, so we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{n}(\mathbf{B}^{1/2}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}^{1/2})=

\displaystyle\max_{x\in\mathbb{R}/\{0\}}\frac{x^{\top}\mathbf{B}^{1/2}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}^{1/2}x}{x^{\top}x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{x\in\mathbb{R}/\{0\}}\Big\{\frac{x^{\top}\mathbf{B}^{1/2}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}^{1/2}x}{x^{\top}\mathbf{B}x}\frac{x^{\top}\mathbf{B}x}{x^{\top}x}\Big\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\min_{y\in\mathbb{R}/\{0\}}\frac{y^{\top}\mathbf{B}y}{y^{\top}y}\max_{x\in\mathbb{R}/\{0\}}\frac{x^{\top}\mathbf{B}^{1/2}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}^{1/2}x}{x^{\top}\mathbf{B}x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(\mathbf{B})\lambda_{n}(\mathbf{A})

and

\displaystyle\lambda_{n}(\mathbf{B}^{1/2}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}^{1/2})=

\displaystyle\max_{x\in\mathbb{R}/\{0\}}\Big\{\frac{x^{\top}\mathbf{B}^{1/2}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}^{1/2}x}{x^{\top}\mathbf{B}x}\frac{x^{\top}\mathbf{B}x}{x^{\top}x}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{y\in\mathbb{R}/\{0\}}\frac{y^{\top}\mathbf{B}y}{y^{\top}y}\max_{x\in\mathbb{R}/\{0\}}\frac{x^{\top}\mathbf{B}^{1/2}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}^{1/2}x}{x^{\top}\mathbf{B}x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda_{n}(\mathbf{B})\lambda_{n}(\mathbf{A}).

So the lemma is proved.

The next lemma is from Theorem 10 in
Tolmatz 2002
:

Lemma D.12
.

Let
B(r)
be a one dimensional standard Brownian motion, then we have

\displaystyle P(\int_{0}^{1}(B(r)-rB(1))^{2}dr\leq\lambda)=2\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}e^{-1/(8\lambda)}[1+O(\lambda)]\mbox{ as }\lambda\to 0+.

(83)

Lemma
D.12
implies that
P(\int_{0}^{1}(B(r)-rB(1))^{2}dr\leq\lambda)
decays faster than
\lambda^{a}
for any
a>0
as
\lambda\to 0+
.

Lemma D.13
.

For a sequence of identically distributed random variables (not necessarily independent)
X_{i}
with
E|X_{i}|^{\alpha}<\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}E\big\{\max\limits_{i\leq n}|X_{i}|^{\alpha}\big\}\to 0

(84)

Proof D.14

(Proof of Lemma
D.13
)
.

Note that for any
\lambda>0
,

\max\limits_{i\leq n}|X_{i}|^{\alpha}\leq\lambda^{\alpha}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}|X_{i}|^{\alpha}\boldsymbol{1}(|X_{i}|>\lambda),

so we have

\frac{1}{n}E\big\{\max\limits_{i\leq n}|X_{i}|^{\alpha}\big\}\leq\frac{\lambda^{\alpha}}{n}+E\big\{|X_{1}|^{\alpha}\boldsymbol{1}(|X_{1}|>\lambda)\big\}.

The conclusion follows by letting
n\to\infty
then
\lambda\to\infty
.

Lemma D.15
.

Let
(B_{1},B_{2})^{\top}
be a bivariate normal vector with covariance matrix

\Sigma=\begin{bmatrix}\gamma_{1}&\rho\sqrt{\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}}\\
\rho\sqrt{\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}}&\gamma_{2}\end{bmatrix}
,
\sigma_{1}^{2}
and
\sigma_{2}^{2}
be two positive constants. Then for
0<C<1
,

\displaystyle P(\Big|\frac{(B_{1})^{2}}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}-\frac{(B_{2})^{2}}{\sigma_{2}^{2}}\Big|<C)\leq 4C_{\rho}(\sqrt{\frac{\sigma^{2}_{1}}{\gamma_{1}}}+\sqrt{\frac{\sigma^{2}_{2}}{\gamma_{2}}})\sqrt{C},

where
C_{\rho}=\frac{1}{4\sqrt{\pi(1-\rho^{2})(1-|\rho|)}}
.

Proof D.16

(Proof of Lemma
D.15
)
.

It follows from Equation (2.6) in
Krishnan 1967
that the density for the distribution of
(\frac{(B_{1})^{2}}{\sigma_{1}^{2}},\frac{(B_{2})^{2}}{\sigma_{2}^{2}})
at
(u,v)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}
is

\displaystyle f_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(u,v)=\frac{{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}}{4\pi\sqrt{(1-\rho^{2})\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}}\sqrt{uv}}e^{-\frac{u\sigma_{1}^{2}\gamma_{2}+v\sigma_{2}^{2}\gamma_{1}}{2(1-\rho^{2})\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}}}\Big[e^{\frac{\rho\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sqrt{uv}}{(1-\rho^{2})\sqrt{\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}}}}+e^{-\frac{\rho\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sqrt{uv}}{(1-\rho^{2})\sqrt{\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}}}}\Big].

(85)

So for
0<\lambda<1
, we have

\displaystyle f_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(v+\lambda,v)

\displaystyle=\frac{{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}}{4\pi\sqrt{(1-\rho^{2})\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}}\sqrt{(v+\lambda)v}}e^{-\frac{(v+\lambda)\sigma_{1}^{2}\gamma_{2}+v\sigma_{2}^{2}\gamma_{1}}{2(1-\rho^{2})\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}}}\Big[e^{\frac{\rho\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sqrt{(v+\lambda)v}}{(1-\rho^{2})\sqrt{\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}}}}+e^{-\frac{\rho\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sqrt{(v+\lambda)v}}{(1-\rho^{2})\sqrt{\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}}}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}{4\pi\sqrt{(1-\rho^{2})\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}}\sqrt{\lambda v}}2e^{-\frac{((1-|\rho|)[(v+\lambda)\sigma_{1}^{2}\gamma_{2}+v\sigma_{2}^{2}\gamma_{1}]}{2(1-\rho^{2})\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}}}

(86)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}{4\pi\sqrt{(1-\rho^{2})\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}}\sqrt{\lambda v}}2e^{-\frac{((1-|\rho|)(\sigma_{1}^{2}\gamma_{2}+\sigma_{2}^{2}\gamma_{1})v}{2\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\sqrt{\lambda v}}\Big[\frac{\sigma^{2}_{1}}{\gamma_{1}}e^{-(1-|\rho|)\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}v}+\frac{\sigma^{2}_{2}}{\gamma_{2}}e^{-(1-|\rho|)\frac{\sigma^{2}_{2}}{\gamma_{2}}v}\Big].

(87)

Here we used the inequality
|2\rho\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{2}}{\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}}(v+\lambda)v}|\leq|\rho|((v+\lambda)\frac{\sigma^{2}_{1}}{\gamma_{1}}+v\frac{\sigma^{2}_{2}}{\gamma_{2}})
in Equation (
86
) and
\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}\sigma_{2}^{2}}{\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}}}2e^{-\frac{((1-|\rho|)[v\frac{\sigma^{2}_{1}}{\gamma_{1}}+v\frac{\sigma^{2}_{2}}{\gamma_{2}}]}{2}}\leq\frac{\sigma^{2}_{1}}{\gamma_{1}}e^{-(1-|\rho|)\frac{\sigma^{2}_{1}}{\gamma_{1}}v}+\frac{\sigma^{2}_{2}}{\gamma_{2}}e^{-(1-|\rho|)\frac{\sigma^{2}_{2}}{\gamma_{2}}v}
in Equation (
87
). So the density of
\frac{(B_{1})^{2}}{\sigma^{2}_{1}}-\frac{(B_{2})^{2}}{\sigma^{2}_{2}}
at
\lambda
is

\displaystyle g_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}f_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(v+\lambda,v)dv

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}\sqrt{\lambda}}\Big[\sqrt{\frac{\pi\sigma_{1}^{2}}{(1-|\rho|)\gamma_{1}}}+\sqrt{\frac{\pi\sigma_{2}^{2}}{(1-|\rho|)\gamma_{2}}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\rho}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}(\sqrt{\frac{\sigma^{2}_{1}}{\gamma_{1}}}+\sqrt{\frac{\sigma^{2}_{2}}{\gamma_{2}}}),

(88)

where
C_{\rho}=\frac{1}{4\sqrt{\pi(1-\rho^{2})(1-|\rho|)}}
. Similar result holds for the density of
\frac{(B_{2})^{2}}{\sigma^{2}_{2}}-\frac{(B_{1})^{2}}{\sigma^{2}_{1}}
, so we have for
0<C<1
,

\displaystyle P(\Big|\frac{(B_{1})^{2}}{\sigma^{2}_{1}}-\frac{(B_{2})^{2}}{\sigma^{2}_{2}}\Big|<C)\leq 4C_{\rho}(\sqrt{\frac{\sigma^{2}_{1}}{\gamma_{1}}}+\sqrt{\frac{\sigma^{2}_{2}}{\gamma_{2}}})\sqrt{C}.

For random variable
X
and
q>2
, let
||X||_{q}=(E|X|^{q})^{1/q}
and
c_{q}
be a generic constant which only depends on
q
and may vary each time it appears. Note that under Assumption
3
,
\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\theta_{n,j,q}\leq C\Theta_{n}
for some positive constant
C
. The following lemma gives a uniform bound on the distance between
\sigma_{ni}^{2}
and its sample version.

Lemma D.17
.

Under Assumption
3
, we have

\displaystyle\max\limits_{i\leq p_{n}}||\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(X_{nk}^{i}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}X_{nj}^{i})^{2}-\sigma_{ni}^{2}||_{q/2}\leq c_{q}n^{-1/2}\Theta_{n}^{2}

(89)

Proof D.18

(Proof of Lemma
D.17
)
.

Since

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(X_{nk}^{i}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}X_{nj}^{i})^{2}-\sigma_{ni}^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}[(X_{nk}^{i}-\mu_{n}^{i})^{2}-\sigma_{ni}^{2}]+\Big\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(X_{nk}^{i}-\mu_{n}^{i})\Big\}^{2},

(90)

we have
||\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(X_{nk}^{i}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}X_{nj}^{i})^{2}-\sigma_{ni}^{2}||_{q/2}\leq||\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}[(X_{nk}^{i}-\mu_{n}^{i})^{2}-\sigma_{ni}^{2}]||_{q/2}{+}||\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(X_{nk}^{i}-\mu_{n}^{i})||_{q}^{2}
.
Note that for
j=0,1,2,\dots
,

\displaystyle||(G_{n}^{i}(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{0}))^{2}{-}(G_{n}^{i}(\boldsymbol{\tilde{\epsilon}}_{0,{-}j}))^{2}||_{q/2}\leq

\displaystyle||(G_{n}^{i}(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{0}))^{2}{-}G_{n}^{i}(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{0})G_{n}^{i}(\boldsymbol{\tilde{\epsilon}}_{0,-j})||_{q/2}{+}||(G_{n}^{i}(\boldsymbol{\tilde{\epsilon}}_{0,-j}))^{2}{-}G_{n}^{i}(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{0})G_{n}^{i}(\boldsymbol{\tilde{\epsilon}}_{0,-j})||_{q/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2||G_{n}^{i}(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{0})||_{q}||G_{n}^{i}(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{0}){-}G_{n}^{i}(\boldsymbol{\tilde{\epsilon}}_{0,-j})||_{q}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\Theta_{n}\theta_{n,j,q}.

By applying Equation (4) of Theorem 1 in
Wu 2007
on the time series
\{(X_{nk}^{i}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}X_{nj}^{i})^{2}-\sigma_{ni}^{2}\}_{k=1}^{n}
and
\{(X_{nk}^{i}-\mu_{n}^{i})\}_{k=1}^{n}
, we have

||\sum_{k=1}^{n}[(X_{nk}^{i}-\mu_{n}^{i})^{2}-\sigma_{ni}^{2}]||_{q/2}\leq c_{q}n^{1/2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}||(G_{n}^{i}(\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{0}))^{2}{-}(G_{n}^{i}(\boldsymbol{\tilde{\epsilon}}_{0,-j}))^{2}||_{q/2}\leq c_{q}n^{1/2}\Theta_{n}^{2}

and
||\sum_{k=1}^{n}(X_{nk}^{i}-\mu_{n}^{i})||_{q}\leq c_{q}n^{1/2}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\theta_{n,j,q}\leq c_{q}n^{1/2}\Theta_{n}
. So
||\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}[(X_{nk}^{i}-\mu_{n}^{i})^{2}-\sigma_{ni}^{2}]||_{q/2}\leq c_{q}n^{-1/2}\Theta_{n}^{2}
,
||\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(X_{nk}^{i}-\mu_{n}^{i})||_{q}^{2}\leq c_{q}n^{-1}\Theta^{2}_{n}
and the lemma is proved.

Lemma D.19
.

For
i=1,2,\dots,n
, let
\chi_{i}
be
n
i.i.d chi-square random variables with one degree of freedom and
0<a_{i}
, then for any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle P(\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\chi_{i}\leq\epsilon\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i})\leq\sqrt{e\epsilon}.

(91)

Proof D.20

(Proof of Lemma
D.19
)
.

Without loss of generality, assume
\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}=1
and for
\lambda>0

\displaystyle P(\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\chi_{i}\leq\epsilon\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i})\leq

\displaystyle Ee^{\lambda(\epsilon-\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\chi_{i})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\lambda\epsilon}\prod_{i=1}^{n}(1+2\lambda a_{i})^{{-}1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{\lambda\epsilon}(1+2\lambda)^{{-}1/2}.

Letting
\lambda=(\epsilon^{-1}-1)/2
, the lemma follows.

Let
\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
be the Borel
\sigma
-algebra on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and for
\mathbb{R}^{2}
-valued random vectors
\mathbf{X},\mathbf{Y}
, let
||\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{Y}||_{TV}=\sup_{A\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}|P(\mathbf{X}\in A)-P(\mathbf{Y}\in A)|
be the total variation distance. The following lemma comes from
Devroye
et al. 2018
.

Lemma D.21
.

For any bivariate normal random vectors
\mathbf{X}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma_{1}),\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\Sigma_{2})
where both
\Sigma_{1}
and
\Sigma_{2}
are positive definite, we have

||\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{Y}||_{TV}\leq 3||\Sigma_{2}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{1}\Sigma_{2}^{-1/2}-I_{2}||_{F},

where
I_{2}
is a 2 dimensional identity matrix and
||\cdot||_{F}
denote the Frobenius norm.
